                             WARNING

   Heavy currents at moderate potentials are carried in the wiring of this
equipment. These currents are dangerous to life.

   All operating adjustments of this equipment are made with the power on.
Be careful when examining the wiring of the equipment.

   When repairing or replacing parts on a particular circuit, remove the
power cord to avoid possible electrocution.

                                          CHAPTER 6

                     FIELD MAINTENANCE INSTRUCTIONS

    Note. This chapter contains information for field maintenance of Telegraph Terminal TH-5/TG. Information
for field maintenance of Telegraph-Telephone Signal Converter TA-182/U is contained in TM 11-2137. There is
no field maintenance performed on the Electrical Filter Assembly F—98/U. The extent of repair which any field
maintenance unit undertakes is limited only by the tools and test equipment available and by the degree of skill of
assigned personnel,

        Section Il. TROUBLE SHOOTING AT FIELD MAINTENANCE LEVEL

    Warning: When servicing Telegraph Terminal TH-5/TG, be extremely careful because of the
high voltages exposed. Keep one hand in a pocket when measuring socket voltages with a probe.
Before touching any part of the equipment after the voltage is turned off, short the part to ground.

90. Trouble-Shooting Procedures                            92. Test Equipment Required for Trouble
                                                                    Shooting
    The first step in servicing a defective compo-
nent is to sectionalize the fault, which means             The test equipment required for trouble
tracing the fault to the major component or cir-
cuit responsible for the abnormal operation of             shooting and the associated technical manuals
the set. The second step is to localize the fault,
which means tracing the fault to the defective             are listed below.
part responsible for the abnormal condition.
Some faults, such as burned-out resistors and              Test equipment     Technical manual
shorted transformers, often can be located by
sight, smell, or hearing. The majority of faults,          Tube Tester I-177 or equal_______ TM 11-2627
however, must be localized by checking voltages
and resistances.                                           Multimeter TS-852/U___________ TM 11-5527

                                     CHAPTER 7

                  SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE
           AND DEMOLITION TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

           Section I. SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE

118. Disassembly                                      119. Repacking for Shipment or Limited
                                                                  Storage
   The following instructions are recommended
as a guide for preparing Telegraph-Telephone             a. The exact procedure for repacking for
Terminal AN/TCC-14 for transportation or              shipment or limited storage depends on the
storage. To disassemble the equipment, re-            material available and the conditions under
verse the procedure for setting up the compo-         which the equipment is to be shipped or stored.
nents (par. 20).                                      Reverse the instructions given in paragraph 19.

   a. Disconnect the power plugs.                        b. Whenever practicable, place a dehydrating
   b. Disconnect all communication wires from         agent, such as silica gel, inside the packing case.
the binding posts.                                    Protect the case with a waterproof paper bar-
   c. Remove the components from Mounting             rier. Seal the seams of the paper with water-
MT-791/U.                                             proof sealing compound or tape.

           Section II. DEMOLITION TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

120. Methods of Demolition                            all equipment to prevent its being used or saly-
                                                      aged by the enemy.
   a. Smash. Use sledges, axes, handaxes, pick-
axes, hammers, crowbars, heavy tools.                    a. Smash (par. 120a) the controls, tubes,
                                                      coils, switches, capacitors, transformers, and
   b. Cut. Use axes, handaxes, machetes.              headsets.
   c. Burn. Use gasoline, kerosene, oil, flame
throwers, incendiary grenades.                           b. Cut (par. 120b) cords, headsets, and
   d. Explode. Use firearms, grenades, TNT.           wiring.
   e. Dispose. Bury in slit trenches, fox holes,
other holes. Throw in streams. Scatter.                  c. Burn (par. 120c) cords, resistors, capaci-
                                                      tors, coils, wiring, and technical manuals.
    Note. Use anything immediately available for de-
struction of this equipment.                             d. Bury or scatter (par. 120e) .all of the
                                                      above parts after they have been rendered use-
121. Destruction of Components      destroy           less.

   When ordered by your commander,                       e. Destroy Everything.

4. Oscillator and Discriminator Capacitor Selection

   Capacitors designated C10, C35, and C37 in paragraph 2 of this appendix   will consist of one
or two of the trimmer capacitors listed in a and b below. Refer to chapter   6 for the selection
procedure.

a. Trimmer capacitors used in the alinement of the oscillator circuit are listed below:

                            Name of part and description                     Signal Corps
                                                                               stock No.
CAPACITOR,  fixed:  silver  mica; 470 uuf +10%; 500 v de; JAN CM20D471K.
CAPACITOR,  fixed:  mica;   1000 wef +5%; 500 v de; JAN CM30D102J.            3K2047141
CAPACITOR,  fixed:  mica;   2000 uuf +5%; 500 v de; JAN CM30D202J.            3K3010241
CAPACITOR,  fixed:  mica;    3900 wef +5%; 500 v de; JAN CM385D392J.          3K3020242
